:REASON:       I will pin one AF integration commit+tarball and add the connector ACK/outbox gate. For reconnect, B bf6e8e0 keeps original connection authority and does not re-resolve: fresh authorize/current-access checks may only allow the same frozen destination; a stale binding stays private with B's internal readable notice (no guessed external notice). Is that the intended meaning of your new reconnect requirement?

:ASK_ID:       ask-69df3419b44a4640b7c88b392b7e1c6b

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 20:17:43 run.answered TASK-E9C1M
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalconnectome-820d6556-f782-467b-9d37-04dda0653488
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 20:17:43]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.connectome-host
:TASK:         TASK-E9C1M
:REASON:       Yes, that's exactly what I mean. Across a reconnect: never re-resolve. The fresh authorize/current-access check can only allow the same frozen destination. A stale binding stays private with B's internal readable notice to the agent. No external notice is invented: an operator notice route is only a proposal in the doc, marked as an owner decision. Uncertain tool effects are never repeated automatically.

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 20:51:45 run.answered TASK-E9C1M
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalconnectome-d14732c0-d786-4d28-bf6e-9be06c5465ca

:PROJECT:      theaspirational.connectome-host
:TASK:         TASK-E9C1M
:REASON:       Yes, write what the sources show, with file:line evidence: the host-side shouldTriggerInference gates wake only, but the patched frozen AF (src/framework.ts:20589-20597, :20726-20744; dist framework.js; source SHA c7c7e53… matching the manifest) calls bridge.accept and refuses !decision.store BEFORE pushHandler/channelRegistry. So the frozen pair HAS a storage guard. In the report, explicitly mark that REVIEW2 M1 is factually wrong on this point and give the evidence. Then the owner decision is NOT "loss of ambient context" in general. Compare exactly the OLD storage rule (when the old bridge returns store:true/false: which channels/conditions, from host-recipient-protocol.ts) with the NEW readChannels → store mapping. If the set of stored items differs in a concrete case (for example channels the old bridge stored without waking but the new readChannels doesn't cover, or the reverse), name exactly that difference as an owner decision, plus a fixture row. If there's no difference with a correct mapping, write "no difference with mapping X" and DON'T add the owner decision; make it a mapping requirement plus a test instead. Don't invent a difference.

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 20:54:18 run.message TASK-E9C1M
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalconnectome-114fd1e5-47e1-4754-8cdf-5c5e8ef4c8a1
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 20:54:18]
:TYPE:         run.message
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.connectome-host
:TASK:         TASK-E9C1M
:REASON:       Addition to my answer on ask-e770599d (from Soma, who independently checked: frozen source SHA c7c7e53e…a31c8; framework.ts:20589–20598 and 20726–20744 refuse !decision.store before the push/channel handlers). In the old/new comparison, keep THREE things separate: read permission, storage, and wake. The synthetic baseline has readJoinedChannels:true. Don't map the old write/wake whitelist onto a narrower new readChannels: a read whitelist must cover all joined channels the old pair read. Fixture rows required: (a) valid ambient context in a joined channel → stored, no wake, on BOTH pairs; (b) an unreadable/invalid envelope → no store, on both. An owner decision appears only for an evidenced old/new effect difference with file:line; the host-only inference from REVIEW2 M1 must not become an owner decision.

:REASON:       Additional conditions for Phase P (Soma; binding): (1) The image build is fully offline: `--pull=never`, `--network=none` during the build too, NO apt/npm/package install and no lifecycle hooks; empty environment (`env -i` plus only what's necessary, listed in the report); no privileges and no changes to the host session/linger/podman config. If a public dependency turns out to be missing, DON'T enable networking; STOP and describe the method of acquiring it in the report. (2) Committed files (the harness) must contain NO operator-specific manifest, no real host paths (/srv, /opt, /home) and no private traceability. The harness takes declared inputs via arguments/relative container mount paths (e.g. /inputs/old, /inputs/new) and is reproducible. The operator manifest with real paths, the argv with host paths and the inventories go ONLY into evidence/report, not into commits. (3) Native CM/Chronicle format and D stamp are separate phases; (b) waits for the A+B+D tarball. EACCES/import/ABI/read-only store is never a pass. The limits proof is a no-op only; Phase E is not run. Confirm these conditions in the report.

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 21:35:34 run.message TASK-MHQNZ
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalconnectome-fb9f532f-4e52-4b36-95de-99c344ce79fd
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 21:35:34]
:TYPE:         run.message
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.connectome-host
:TASK:         TASK-MHQNZ
:REASON:       Additional requirement for revision 2 (Soma, binding): EVIDENCE CAPTURE. /work tmpfs disappears when the container stops, so "podman cp later" isn't acceptable. Do it like this: the harness streams evidence to STDOUT as bounded, framed records (one JSON line per event/inventory/result, each with its own sha256, plus a running chained hash), flushing after every attempt, so that on a timeout or kill the partial evidence is already outside the container. At the end, a final record with the total count and chain hash. The host-side launcher (launch.sh) writes stdout/stderr to a file in evidence via redirection (no host RW mount into the container) and then verifies the chain/count with a separate checker; any break/truncation = inconclusive. Total stdout size bounded (state the cap, e.g. ≤256MiB, and what happens when it's exceeded: refuse plus a final record). Traces (strace) go through the same channel (or are summarized inside the container and streamed out), not left in tmpfs. NO RW volume mounts. Demonstrate it in the no-op proof: a mock stream of a few records plus a forced kill in the middle → the checker shows the partial evidence and marks it inconclusive. In the report, label Node rows as a "native-format component probe", not as old-reader proof.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M67BPB295V5D0XX7A2BKCTVQ
:MESSAGE_ID:   msg-a2f8cef559094c7aa4c447af3dd3bb27
:FROM:         member:tarn2
:DELIVERY:     delivered
:EVENT_ID:     a1aeafef-f452-4947-ba57-cf7378080fea
:END:

:REASON:       More binding conditions for revision 2 (Soma): (1) COMPLETENESS = a normal terminal record + the expected case count (declared in advance, per runtime×route×scenario) + a host-side exit-status/deadline receipt from launch.sh. Without all three, the result is INCOMPLETE. On a hard kill there may be no terminal record: keep the verified prefix and label it incomplete; NEVER synthesize a closing record on the host. (2) Traces: chunks or summaries must keep enough syscall/path/timing detail (syscall, path, flags/mode, result, a relative timestamp, pid) to correlate exposure/mutation with the refusal point. A hash of discarded trace bytes is NOT evidence. If the volume exceeds the cap, filter to file syscalls on the store paths, but don't replace them with a hash. (3) In the report: the hash chain proves integrity/truncation, not that the intended experiment happened; state that explicitly. Show the no-op/kill receipts: a mock stream, then a kill in the middle → the checker gives INCOMPLETE with the prefix preserved.

:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalconnectome-369d564a-7d50-4355-b4c2-9938525f008d

:PROJECT:      theaspirational.connectome-host
:TASK:         TASK-KY73N
:REASON:       Binding refinements for M2 (from Soma): an "accepted registration" by itself is NOT authoritative parenthood. lookup() must be bound to the actual connection/epoch/registration generation AND to an independently verified native/attested parent relationship; claimed descriptor fields (parent/label/address/metadata) remain inert. Keep the post-await/stale-epoch negatives and the direction of native identity (native facts → verifiedScope, not the other way round) explicit in docs and fixtures. M1: preserve dynamic WAKE as well as read/storage; any narrowing only as a named contract decision (don't propose one). M4: explicitly SUPERSEDE the incapable exact-list config, don't pretend it covers joined/DM/dynamic. M3: a real gate. M5: private tokens outside the publishable tree; scan the checker itself. LOWs bounded, no new infrastructure. In the report, the refined AF-scope API/acceptance tests are clarifications of the existing conditional AF-scope item (8–16 h), not a new item; static API-gap review ≠ implementation/runtime qualification.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M67FRDPNZHR2ZWVWECNPX3ES

:REASON:       A clarification of the grant point (from Soma), for the verdict: distinguish two paths and do NOT accept one blanket rule "tools grant, not publish" for both: (1) native MCP server send tools (the adapter's own tool, called via a tools/call) and (2) the host's generic `channel_publish` / `channels.publish` path. For each path, find in A 869bb3ad / B 29edcf8b (git show) the actual call path and the effective grant checks (file:line), plus native destination/access policy. A tools grant is not permission widening or owner consent; descriptor claims supply neither. If the new sentence/fixture in bcf28f0e diverges from the code, that's a finding (fix the sentence/fixture, not the permissions) with a severity assessment. No broad audit.

:REASON:       Binding refinements (from Soma): (1) Describe the two send paths exactly as traced; DON'T invent a call-time CapabilityGrant check for native tools. Tool exposure/allow policy and native destination/effect policy are distinct from owner consent, and from each other. (2) The narrow-wake case goes ALONGSIDE the default old-wake parity (don't remove the A04 parity); the claimed-parent/native-agreement case must kill the removal of that conjunct (show the oracle mutation). Don't delete any existing contract case to make the oracle green; if you change one, give the reason in the report. (3) Hygiene: the public functional CI step must NOT depend on private names or a new repository secret; without the list, an honest PARTIAL with exit 0. A separate required publication-hygiene gate (our dispatch gate before publication) may demand a non-empty explicit list and refuse without it. Never write "full hygiene" PASS where the list wasn't supplied. No scope expansion.

Gates: --self-test, --docs-contract (add the new files to the scan if the checker allows it without logic changes; otherwise note that), git diff --check, all bounded. Finalize as done (an input with named blockers), not blocked, unless even this is impossible. LOCAL ONLY, no push.

:TASK:         TASK-6JAEQ
:REASON:       Task operator Tarn's answer (with Soma's condition, programme qualifier; not the owner): CONFIRMED — do NOT impose the push ceiling. I checked generic-recipient-host.md at 9c6f6ea1: "Inference can be explicitly allowed by lane, with no channel reply intent" — a separate documented rule, and borrowing the push rule could cut off legitimate explicit/scheduled requests. Do: (1) documented exemption on the CompiledRecipientAdmissionPolicy interface (:16-24) and in recipient-policy-staging.md: for inference/request the ceiling is ONLY the explicit lane allowance (+ the common key/epoch/abort guard and any existing feature-grant check, if present in the common guard — don't remove it); read/wake/wakeWithoutChannel/addressed don't apply; no reply intent/destination — with citations host:204-208 and T1:229-233. The doc must NOT advertise "all-lane ceilings" to T2. (2) Discriminating controls: (a) deny-all policy WITHOUT inference in lanes → refused, no store/wake; (b) with inference in lanes, but read/wake=false and wakeWithoutChannel=false → store/mayWake true (pins the documented exemption); (c) wrong key/stale epoch/abort → refused; (d) no replyIntent on any path. (3) Mutants killed: unconditional true BEFORE the lane/key/abort guard; dropping the lane check; and a "push-ceiling borrow" mutant (applying wakeWithoutChannel&&addressed) — it must be killed by control (b). (4) Profile-off/unconfigured inference unchanged — byte-identical in the feature-off probe. (5) If you find that the "maintenance/scheduled" meaning of inference in H/T1/A is defined differently than in host:204-208 — record it as a contract gap, don't decide. LOW 1–4 as in the brief.
